And at about the eleventh hour of the night on the two hundred steps, a docker of fifty-eight who cannot read asks him whether two shapes on a slate are the same, and he tells her the truth — that it is the same shape and he cannot tell her they say the same thing — and she says **that is the first honest answer anybody has given me about them in nine years**, and does not ask him again, and goes down to the lock at the top of the tide because it is Tuesday's work and the forty men are going under the near gate in ice in about four hours.

## Volume 02, Batch 0003 — Chapters 71–80: The Shrine (the volume midpoint)

### Chapter 0071 — Four Days
Day 71, the Tuesday, the forty-sixth day of the new month, four days before the review. **The four who hold the door go down the condemned rail in ice with a gauge and do not agree — three at a line and Peth Lomas a sixteenth under, the second time, entered and not deleted — and the twenty-fifth reading is not taken and is not going to be.** Renna Ord's reason for going is fifteen words and is the standing rule of the fortieth day of this flood turned the other way. **Orla Specht, sixty-one, of Silt Row, who cannot read, has written the review's second page out nine times in nine hands in nine days, at her own cost in labour, and she is not sorry**, and the girl of seventeen finds nine hands in a room of eleven. **The copy is not theft and is not a conspiracy and a notary of fifty asked for the copies herself**, and her eleven words are the finding: a document nobody may copy is a document nobody may check. She also enters that she cannot un-ring it. **A number in a question is a photograph**, and the count on the fourth of the eleven is corrected from sixty-eight days to seventy-one in front of nine people, struck and not deleted; and nobody in this city has ever written down the fortieth day of this flood, which nine mouths use as an anchor. Adrian admits he has been calling the eleventh question his for two days and that the four words on the back of the slate are a docker's. **A man in the lower gallery reads all eleven aloud in an empty gallery, badly on purpose, four of them wrong, and is not going to be in the room on Saturday** — because one of the nineteen reasons on the second page says this city is not going to ask him what the next one will be, so that he is not to be called in.

### Chapter 0072 — What Are They Called
Day 72, the Wednesday, the forty-seventh day of the new month, three days out. **Nine people in nine rooms are asked what the eleventh question will be answered with, and all nine answers are true and none of them is the answer**: a woman of sixty-one says *the ones the door costs*, a docker says *workers*, a Hearthguard says *the dead*, a woman of twenty-four says *people the door costs*, a man of forty-four says *my grandfather*, a man of thirty-one says *strangers*, a notary declines, a docker of fifty-eight declines, and a girl of sixteen on no roll says she would like to keep it that way. **A girl of seventeen, on a pail, in about nine minutes: the question is not asking for a noun, it is asking for the heading of a column, and you only ask somebody for a heading when you have already decided there is going to be a column.** The room refuses a proposed heading in thirty-five words. **The shape of the answer is already in this city in four lines on a slate on the nail, put up on the forty-first day of the new month by a keeper of seventy — the nineteenth line, which says what may be done to a person and contains no word for what a person is.** The not-finding is entered in nine lines. **And at about the seventh hour of the evening, in a yard, in about two hundred and forty people, Renna Ord is asked what she thinks the answer is and says four words, and they are not a noun, and the girl of seventeen chalks them on the front of the slate with a woman's hand over a girl's fingers, and the question they answer is on the back of it, and about nine feet of weather apart, and nobody turns it over.**

### Chapter 0073 — The Twenty-Second Field
Day 73, the Thursday, the forty-eighth day of the new month, two days out. **A girl of seventeen is written on a page with a date for the first time in this volume** and asks what that is, and a notary of fifty tells her it is a page and not a roll and then enters that she does not know whether she is safe. **The twenty-second field arrives in a yard behind a chandlery and it is the twenty-first again with the condition taken off**: the district may be described as settling its accounts in kind, the description may be published, and the district will not be charged a fee again. About nine people say the twenty-first was right and this one is harmless. **A second clerk of the Registry says, in about nine seconds, in the local language, that it is not harmless, and that a body with a standing is a body that may be written to, and that a district which has been given a permanent exemption is the only body in ninety years of his office's forms that could be written to without anybody's name being on it — and asks for it to be entered that he said it about his own office.** **Adrian refuses in under a second, out loud, before he knows who it is for, in three parts, and gives the third part back to the clerk because the sentence is his.** The count of refusals in the second book goes to **twenty-two**; Sallow's own book goes to **thirteen**; the difference of nine is entered and has never been explainable. **Priya Shah: a form that repeats itself is not asking twice, it is establishing that it may** — fifteen words, on the back of a copying list. And Adrian enters, at her request, nineteen words: **I have never once asked what happens to the ones that are not answered by me.**

### Chapter 0074 — The Nineteenth Line
Day 74, the Friday, the forty-ninth day of the new month, the day before the review. **Dante Kuo's second scene, and it is about the nineteenth line and not about his shoulder.** He had four days' notice and used them to ask somebody else first: a foreman who has not gone under the near gate since the twenty-fifth day of this flood says **no**, and the answer is not a fault and nobody argues, and the nineteenth line is then **amended for the worse at his own asking** to carry *I can do some and not others* — the first time in seventy-four days that a line on that board has been changed by the person the line is about. When he is asked himself, he refuses to be a man who is one fact. **A docker's apprentice of twenty, a name nobody in a yard has said out loud, says no, and the day's work is entered as not done**, and the fourth page is used for the second time and works: *a man who is asked and says no is not a man who did not turn up.* **Nobody thanked the man who said no and nobody thanked the man who asked.** The room then counts the roll of the forty and finds **thirty-eight names doing the work and two that are not**, and Renna Ord's four turns out to be a fraction of a number that is not the number, and her finding is twenty-two words: **a prediction which is wrong in the direction it wanted to be is worse than one that is wrong the other way.** Her nine words about what the nineteenth line cost: *it cost a day's work and bought a record.* And a man of twenty asks his third question and gets four sentences about nineteen shapes, about a docker copying what she cannot read, about a slate anybody may read in the rain, and about not being told a fourth thing is coming.

### Chapter 0075 — What Are They Called: The Review
Day 75, the Saturday, the fiftieth day of the new month. **The review, in daylight, in a converted salt store, in about two hundred and forty people, against eleven questions, a second page and a slate.** The four went down the condemned rail in ice and **agreed**, for the first time in seventy-five days, and the twenty-fifth reading was not taken. The first question is a door; the second is the notary's own cost column, opened in daylight for the first time in twenty-six years, and the gap is named in full; the third is the trap, and its answer is the fourth line of a term sheet and the ninth household of Nine Elms, and **the instrument is continued in a form weaker than the one it went in with.** The second page is read in the wrong order on purpose by a keeper of seventy. **Then the eleventh question is read, and the answer is given, and it is four words, and it is not a noun, and it is *nobody has asked them*, and it was on the front of the slate for three days while the question was on the back, and nobody turned it over.** It is entered in a column on the same page as the question, not on a separate sheet, and read back twice in two languages. The room then spends ninety seconds trying to find out who *they* is and **cannot produce a single name**, and the answer stands. **The weakness is the finding: a licence survived because a city said no, and a city that says no says it in a room.** A Crown letter is not answered. Nine Elms' water is not received. **A seat is offered a fourth time, twenty-five days after the third, and the instrument says nobody again** — moved by a Hearthguard who wanted it in the record that he moved it and lost it. **The last column of the Crown schedule, headed *person to be asked*, is answered with a question and not with a name**, and a clerk of that registry says, in about nine seconds: that is not a person. And at about the eighth hour of the evening, four feet back from a river wall, a man of thirty-two reads a number off a dressed join and says it out loud, and the number is a number and not a sentence about anybody, and there is no field in the air.

### Chapter 0076 — The Form of the Continued Instrument
Day 76, the Sunday, the fifty-first day of the new month, the day after. **A notary of fifty says in nine words that the instrument she wrote is worse than the one she was asked to write**, and the whole day is the argument about whether worse and honest are the same thing. The difference is found in nine minutes: the old instrument had a figure-shaped hole and the new one has the hole described. Adrian says in thirty-two words that a description is the only thing in this world that has never cost anybody anything until an office reads it, and asks that it not be entered by the man it is about. **Somebody proposes three and it is refused in about nine minutes by a docker of about sixty who is sent for**, on the standing sentence from the fortieth day of this flood: *four was a quorum and two is an opening.* **The first line added to an instrument in seventy-six days for the benefit of a person who is not a witness**: the person this instrument costs may be asked, once, on a named day, in daylight, in a room, and the answer may be no. **A line with no date in it is a standing, and a standing cannot be refused**, so a date is argued out in about four minutes, and it is a Sunday, and it is a market day, and it is seven days from now. **Renna Ord is entered as somebody who may also ask the question**, in sixteen words, and asks for the other three of the nine to be left alone. The cost column is entered for the tenth time in one month and has no figure, and **the tenth entry is smaller than the ninth and a smaller entry is not a cheaper column.** And at about the ninth hour of the evening a keeper reads the new line out in an empty yard to nine people, one of them a docker of fifty-eight who cannot read, who asks whether it is on the slate, and is told it is on the page and not on the slate, and that this is on purpose, because a page on a nail is a page people read and a page in a stone is a page people have to come and ask for.

### Chapter 0077 — The Fifth Document
Day 77, the Monday, the fifty-second day of the new month. **A case comes up the river from Aurel and crossed in the middle of the water with the case that went down on the Friday carrying the second page, and neither can be said to have overtaken the other.** It holds **the fifth thing that office has put in a case for this district and the fourth shape it has sent**, and the room confuses those two counts for about nine minutes. It is a schedule of crossings in the form required with one more column than the last, and the column is headed **receiving party** — the condition the licence's own second page has carried since the twenty-fifth day of the new month, written into a Crown table and called a column. **A woman of twenty-four asks the question the whole volume turns on in ten words: they have written down a thing we cannot fill in.** A clerk of the Registry says that in ninety years the column has been completed **eleven times, nine of them garrisons, one a customs house with a garrison in it, one a chartered company, and never a village**, and that nobody has ever been able to find out whether that is because no village has agreed or because nobody has ever asked a village — and then, in twelve words: **none of those eleven ever asked a person on the far side.** The honest useless form must be re-entered and cannot be entered the same way twice, and about nine people work out that a document designed to be unhelpful can be made more unhelpful without becoming a lie: **it carries no names, and the receiving-party cell carries a cross in a notary's hand and nineteen words underneath — no receiving party has been asked and none has agreed and this is not the same as none existing.** **An incomplete return is a real procedure, and a hearing is a date, and the date is in about eleven weeks**, and a docker of fifty-eight says the only sentence anybody says: *a date is a thing I have been asking this city for since the twenty-sixth.* And in a yard a man asks a man in a good coat a plain question, and gets the best answer anybody has given in this city in ten days, and it is not a sentence about a door: **I do not know. I have been a clerk nineteen years and I have written four hundred of these and I could tell you in nine seconds what every other one was for, and I cannot tell you what that one is for — and I am going to answer it anyway.**

### Chapter 0078 — A Work With No Office
Day 78, the Tuesday, the fifty-third day of the new month. **Perry Lask, forty-one, the only person in this world who holds a workway, does a work there is no office for**, on a resonant place with a boundary cut into it, nine miles up the upper road, in the wrong weather, in the wrong company, having told nobody, and **there is no room, and there is no procedure, and there is no office, and nobody is in a position to ask her anything until a Sunday five days from now, because the line Adrian fought two hours and ten minutes for is the thing standing between her and every question anybody has got — and she says so in eleven words and asks for it on the same page as the line.** A boundary stone is a join. **A workway is for a promise**, and nobody in this city had said it out loud in seventy-eight days, and what she did was answer a promise a river made about where the water was, a hundred and forty years ago, not made to her and not heard by anybody. **Mina Okoye's sentence is the load-bearing rule and it is hers and it is said by her, in a room, in about nine seconds, about a person who is not her, and nobody claimed it and it is entered that nobody did.** The working has **two of the four things and not three** — a pattern a hundred and forty years old and her hand — because **nobody was asked**, and a person standing in a working who has not been asked has not agreed to it and is therefore not a witness. Adrian is four feet back, is barred from nothing, and does nothing. **The twenty-third field arrives and offers the holder's work an office, and nobody answers it, and he does not refuse it, and enters the reason in thirty-six words: he has told this city for seventy-eight days that a rule about a thing is not a rule about the world, and he is not going to be a man who answers for a room.** A bone-setter and a woman of twenty-nine can find nothing on the arm, and the finding is twenty-two words: she will not die and she will not be whole and nobody will be able to say what was wrong with her. **The only instrument in this city that could have asked her what she did is the twentieth field, which says nobody may be asked to describe the holder's work**, and a consequence is written on the back of it in four hands. And a boy of nine asks his third question and gets an answer about a word on a wall that has not been written down in nine years, and a private-book line about him: a nine-year-old has worked out in nine seconds what this city has failed at for nine years.

### Chapter 0079 — A Rule About a Thing
Day 79, the Wednesday, the fifty-fourth day of the new month. **The volume's midpoint, and the failure is Adrian's.** He convenes it himself, in daylight, with a date on the notice in his own hand and a line nobody can refuse, and gets it in about eleven minutes: **the bar says a workway may not be offered by this instrument or by anybody using it; it is the longest page in the licence; it is not broken; it did not reach; a rule that covers a document is a rule about a document; nobody used anything and there was no hand on any lever; the page is perfect and does not reach.** **The thing it did not reach was a person, on a boundary, on the fifty-third day of the new month, and he was four feet back.** About ninety seconds in, a defence is offered him and he refuses it in four seconds and asks for the refusal to be entered, and a woman of forty-one says in a yard: do not be a man who is excused in a yard. **Then a woman of thirty-one of Nine Elms uses the fourth line of a term sheet against him correctly**, having walked eleven miles to do it, and tells him that a man is not one person, and names a woman of forty-eight in the fourth house at Nine Elms who has been clearing a drain by herself every morning since the twenty-ninth day of the new month because the levy that used to do it is gone, **and then says the twenty-four words that are the shape of a rescue: a rescue is a working, a working needs a pattern, a floor that has come open once is a hole and not a pattern.** He cannot answer it. They go up to a shrine on a boundary at Four Elms and **nothing happens there and he wanted it to.** He answers with a page: the whole of the charge is on one man, a man is not one person, therefore it is to be asked separately, in daylight, in a room, of each person the charge is made of, and **no person is named on the page.** **Wenna Cray enters the fifth statement of error in the history of the book, four sentences, every one correct**, and he is not permitted to answer it, because the third clause is a real procedure and using it is correct, and the one he uses is the one that requires no sentence from him: *I stand by it. All four are correct. I am not going to answer a word of it today.* **A Hearthguard of forty-four says two sentences in that yard — a rule that only covers the thing you can see is a rule about a thing and not about the world, and a guild that does not go home — and the first has been said four times about other people and this is the first time either has been said about the man who wrote the page**, and a clerk of the Registry in a very good coat carries a bench, confirms the shape in one word, and hands the city the only thing it will ever have against him. **Nobody says the word *resign*, because there is nothing to resign from, and a man with nothing to resign is not a man being let go.**

### Chapter 0080 — The Shrine
Day 80, the Thursday, the fifty-fifth day of the new month, five days to March. **A page goes on the inside of a shrine on a boundary at Four Elms that somebody else put there, and it is the first page in this city put somewhere that is not this city's building.** A shrine is not an instrument and nothing may be worked at it and there is no pattern in it; **it is a record, and the cut in the flat of the stone says where the water was before the boundary was moved a hundred yards downstream at a public act with witnesses, and it has been saying so for a hundred and forty years and it is not on paper.** Marda Rood, about sixty-one, of Four Elms, keeps it and gives the batch's best argument for a stone: *every other thing in this valley that remembers anything is on paper, and paper goes in a case and a case comes back empty.* The page carries six things, with the price — a quarter of a pound of salt a month cut out of the keeping household's own winter allotment — **written under the six and not a seventh**, and the sixth says anybody may come to the stone and ask what it is at the door and be answered there. **The keeping household was asked to have its name on it and would not: a name on a page is a thing that can be found, and I have buried two.** Nobody stepped over it. **The Reed Commons hardens**: Hessa Ruun reads out the meeting-ground's answer in nineteen minutes — the page is refused, the fourth line stands, the charge stays on the private debtor — and the reason is that a page that asks four people is a description, and in four months a man in Aurel will read four names off the back of it; and a woman of twenty-four costs it in order, second sentence first, and the page is refused and correct. At about the sixth hour the licence, the door, the fee, the field and the review are all read out in about two hundred and forty people and none of it is good news, and **nobody turned the slate over, because the question was a good question and the only thing this city has never wanted is a good question turned over.** And at about the ninth hour of the evening, at the top of the upper road, a woman of sixty-one who cannot read reads nineteen shapes aloud to a docker who has a slate, because anybody may pick the work up; eight of the nineteen are different and further down the road and have not been looked at; **one of them is not a shape, it is a name, in a hand about a hundred and forty years old, and nobody has looked at the other seven** — and it is not tonight, and the word is a page and a page has a date on it and a price under it and somebody who can be angry at it.

## Volume 02, Batch 0004 — Chapters 81–90: The False Oath

### Chapter 0081 — The Roll and the Case
Day 81, the Friday, the fifty-sixth day of the new month, five days to the first of March. **A case goes down to a wharf with the eleven questions, the second page, the four words and the four words, two honest useless forms and a strip of paper about what the office has been told — and the roll of strangers goes with a hole in it**, the hole entered as a place where a number used to be. Peth Lammay's thirteen-word standing sentence is said before she has taken her coat off and the finding of the day is hers: **a case with nothing in it is a case nobody can charge to carry, so the emptiest thing in the case is the safest thing in it.** A notary of fifty sends the roll on the fifty-sixth day with one reason, sixteen words long. **A woman of thirty-eight who is on no roll at all is asked one thing by a woman of twenty-nine and not by Adrian Vale, and says a daughter's name and its spelling out loud in a room, in two languages, to a person who wrote it down, and says the second half of the cost herself — a name on a page is a thing that can be found — before anybody can, and says she chose the room and not the sea.** Adrian asked not to be in the room, gave five words for why, and was told he had no vote; his whole response is thirteen words. **The fee is entered for the twenty-second time with no date, the salt is not sent, a girl of seventeen says the number out loud, and nobody in the room says the word *budget*.**

### Chapter 0082 — Then Ask Me on the Day
Day 82, the Saturday, the fifty-seventh day of the new month, and the asking on the Sunday morning in the fifty-eighth. **A bone-setter finds nothing for the fourth time and says the only thing anybody can do about it is ask her.** A notary of fifty discovers at about the fourth hour of the afternoon that this city has arranged a question for a woman without once asking her whether there was going to be one, and stops everything for about eleven minutes. **The four things decided on the fortieth day of the new month are put in an order on purpose — three refusals first, the question last — and the answer and the refusals go on the same line, because they are the same decision.** Adrian Vale is not allowed to ask and says so first; the notary gives three reasons and enters that he wanted the third and is not going to have it. **Dante Kuo, thirty-five, whose shoulder cannot be set, asks it, on the Sunday, in daylight, in a room, standing where he can see the door, and says in nineteen words that being useful is a description and not a safety.** She answers in nine words: **a river promised a stone. I am the woman** — the first description of her own work anybody in this world has ever had, hers, may be taken back on the date, and the ninth word is the word for a person.

### Chapter 0083 — The Field, Two Days Out
Day 83, the Sunday, the fifty-eighth day of the new month, two days to the first of March. **Elsa Skell, forty-four, of Corrow, one of the four who read the join-line and the woman who has not spoken to Adrian Vale since the eighteenth day of this flood, gives a number, a date and three sentences about a man and one about a field.** The third of them is that **nobody in eighty-three days has asked her whether she wants the field**, and she says she does not know. She refuses his offer to ask her first in one sentence, because a man who asks her so that he can write down that he asked is a man who asked a woman a question in a ditch. **The cost column is entered for the eleventh time in one month and has no figure on the end of it**, and a girl of seventeen gets an entry saying this city decided not to fix something and did not forget. The chapter ends with a man from another world standing in a ditch on purpose and being told, in about four sentences, that it is not what he came for.

### Chapter 0084 — In the Lantern Concord's Own Words
Day 84, the Monday, the fifty-ninth day of the new month, one day to the first of March. **A document arrives in this city's own words and it is offered by a man who is not the Crown's and is not the instrument's: Ivenn Marrow, who brings it, reads the dates out, and says whose sentences are in it.** The undertaking of the Lantern Concord, seven clauses and a date, is entered in four minutes as a document and not an instrument. It offers a body of about a hundred and forty people permanent holder of the licence in exchange for taking the whole standing charge off a private debtor, at no cost to anybody — and **every clause in it is a clause somebody in this room wrote in the last eighty-four days.** It is read aloud in a chapel in a salt town called Dray to about two hundred and forty people: **about nine understand it and two hundred and thirty hear a document.** **It is neither refused nor accepted, and the reason it is not refused is discovered in about nine minutes by a stall-keeper and is the worst thing anybody has said about a document in this city: it is the most durable answer anybody in eighty-four days has been offered.** Adrian wants to call it a false oath, is told he does not know the difference, and says the other thing instead — **a sentence somebody has heard is not a promise to that person any more.** Tamsin Quill's line from the walk-round closes the day.

### Chapter 0085 — The First of March
Day 85, the Tuesday, the sixtieth day of the new month. **The season of the bottom field is settled in at ninety-one acres out of a hundred and forty, said once, in daylight, in a gateway, by a woman who has said in three days that nobody asked her whether she wanted it.** Forty unpaid men go under the near gate and turn up, and the oldest sentence in this city goes on a page for the first time at a docker's own asking. **Renna Ord's sentence of the twenty-first day of the new month is said in a room for the first time in sixty-four days, and she says she did not say it for sixty-four days because every time she did somebody went and fixed a thing, and today there is no lever.** The four go down a condemned rail in ice and **do not agree about the dressed edge for the third time in a fortnight, and the twenty-fifth reading is not taken and one ruled line is left in the column.** A woman of twenty-four says the column is not a column but a sentence with a number missing out of the middle of it, and this city has been arguing with the wrong noun for eighty-five days. **A person is named in it in five words in his own hand — the charge is on me — and a boy of nine runs it eleven miles.** The join stops being worked and the day's work is entered as not done. At the eighth hour he reads **eight and seven-eighths** off a wall four feet back and tells a woman of fifty-eight the truth: it has not moved, and the field has.

### Chapter 0086 — The False Oath, Read in a Room
Day 86, the Wednesday, the sixty-first day of the new month. **The undertaking is read properly, three times, clause by clause, in a converted salt store, in daylight, in about two hundred and forty people.** In the ninth minute of the second reading **Garret Slee, sixty-one, who has kept the Lantern Concord's bound book of this city's words for nineteen years and whose name is not at the top of anything, stands up and says nine words: *I never had a man of mine to lose.*** A woman of twenty-four establishes that he was never asked to be the holder of anything and copied it out anyway, and that the document's seventh clause says no person's name appears at the top of it and the bottom line says a hand that is not named on it. **The twenty-fourth field is offered — a holding may be held by a body of persons instead of by a person, nobody in the body is asked separately, the cost is entered, there is no further cost — and nobody refuses it and nobody accepts it, and the count in the second book kept at home does not go up.** The batch's finding belongs to a girl of seventeen, on no roll, in about nine minutes: **the document does not say what the people the door costs are called, and it does not have to, because it costs nobody anything, and a document which does not have to answer the eleventh question has already answered it.** The document goes on a nail in the lock-chapel arch beside the form, and about nine people find out that an arch is a place and a place is a room.

### Chapter 0087 — A Guild That Does Not Go Home
Day 87, the Thursday, the sixty-second day of the new month, at a boat-yard at Long Water. **Jonah Pike, forty-two, walks nine miles to be in the room, says he is not here to sell it, and is asked what he would do with it, and is right — and the tenth part of his answer is a docker's nine words about a lock that a guild can close, said in a yard on the tenth day of the new month, which he arrived at from the other end twenty-eight days later without knowing.** Adrian enters that he tried to get round him for forty-two days and failed and that the failure is the finding. **A man of about forty-four who keeps a stall finds the clause in about nine minutes, on the authority of four undertakings he has signed and one he was wrong about: an undertaking is not in force until a person stands at the top of it and answers for it alone, and the document has no person at the top of it, and that is either the safety or the hole and he is not going to choose.** **Ilda Marsh, seventy, of the fourth house at Nine Elms, is on the step, and says the second clause means her village gets its water and its quarter back at no cost to her, and that a favour you cannot refuse is not a favour, and then says twenty-four words that are the finding of the day: a guild that can be asked has a standing, and a standing cannot be refused, and I know it because we have refused three.** Nobody in the room says the word legal and about nine of them have it in their heads. A notary of fifty enters the count of the Crown channel, forty-seven days from the fortieth day of this flood, on her own initiative.

### Chapter 0088 — A Shape With Three Descriptions
Day 88, the Friday, the sixty-third day of the new month. **The undertaking is not withdrawn, and the first thing anybody says about it is that it is not being withdrawn.** A Hearthguard of forty-four says all three descriptions of the shape about a document for the first time in a yard in daylight in about nine minutes — a rule that only covers the thing you can see, a standing that cannot be refused, a guild that does not go home — and the yard does not go quiet between them. Adrian cannot answer, because he holds no office, no seat, no workway, no mark, no land right and no class privilege, and says so in his own twenty-one words. **He then says out loud, in about two hundred and forty people, that all three descriptions are correct and that the correct ones are the problem, and is asked to say it again, and says it again worse, and both readings are entered side by side with neither above the other.** A notary of fifty enters, as what a person said, **that a document with no person in it can be argued about only by people who have time**, and declines to enter it twice. About nine people in the yard are given a thing to be angry at that is not a person. A stall-keeper then asks what he would do instead, and the answer is nineteen words: **put a person at the top of it, and make that person able to say no, out loud, here.** A man of forty-two says on the page that the proposal on the table is the old form of the oath with a person in it, and that the man who proposed it will do it. The nineteen words go on a slate on a nail in the arch, which now has a form, two slates and a page, and a keeper of about seventy says: **that is not a wall, that is a Wednesday.**

### Chapter 0089 — A Document That Cannot Be Angry At
Day 89, the Saturday, the sixty-fourth day of the new month. **A notary of fifty says her eleven words for the third time, aimed at a document and not at nine copies, and a woman of twenty-four points out that a sentence said twice is a mechanism and a girl of seventeen observes that a room which has heard a sentence has not answered it.** **A woman of about thirty-four who keeps a stall by the wharf and whose name is not on anything, including the four names on the levy of the first Wayhouse, stands up and says nine sentences in about four minutes, and they are the reason the document cannot be checked and the reason it cannot be refused, and those are the same reason, because it was built entirely out of sentences this city had already agreed to.** The twenty-fifth field is offered — a form about a person may be drawn without telling the person — in the middle of a room drafting exactly that form, about nobody, with nobody present to refuse it; nobody refuses it, nobody accepts it, and the count in the second book does not go up. **A form of four lines that would make the document refusable is drafted in about eleven minutes; a notary asks who is it about; about four people try and nobody can answer; it is entered as an unfinished thing.** The undertaking goes to Aurel unsigned and unrefused with a date on it, in a case that carries a review, and a stall-keeper reads the four lines out in a yard and a girl of seventeen says, from the back, the twenty-eight words that are the reason this city is going to have trouble in about four months.

### Chapter 0090 — What Is Still Owed
Day 90, the Sunday, the sixty-fifth day of the new month. **Ten days are read out in about nineteen minutes in a converted salt store and none of it is good news and all of it is entered**: the licence forty days old and weaker, the door shut, the fee owing with no date for the thirty-first time, Nine Elms' water not received, the whole of the standing charge on a man of thirty-two, a field settled in at ninety-one acres and not paid, a document on a river, a hearing coming, a woman of forty-one not whole, a woman of forty-eight not asked. **A second page goes inside the shrine on the north side of a fork at Four Elms: what is owed, owed to named people and not to a body, dated, with a quarter of a pound of salt a month written under it and not as another item**, and three people step over it and a boy of nine does not. **A notary of fifty enters the cost of it — twelve named persons is a column for where twelve people are, cut into a stone whose keeper is nobody's servant — and says in nine seconds that it is the best argument anybody has made in ninety days and that she has made it about a stone.** The batch closes on a different image on the same road: **in daylight, at the second hour of the afternoon, all nineteen shapes are in four slates that four different people can read out loud, eleven are the same and eight are different, one of the eight is a name in a Hearthguard's hand about a hundred and forty years old, the other seven have never been looked at, and the nineteenth day of the month after this one is on a slate and is the only thing on it.** **A girl of seventeen asks whether she may write down what she thinks the eight of them mean, and a docker of fifty-eight who cannot read says no, in daylight, and the reason is that a wall is the cheapest thing in this city and there is already one.**

## Volume 02, Batch 0005 — Chapters 91–100: The Void, Two Houses and a Road

### Chapter 0091 — The Name, in a Room, with a Date on It
Day 91, the Monday, the first day of the month after this one. **A man of thirty-two establishes that the four best things this city has built in ninety-one days cannot be pointed at a document, because all four are about a person and a document with no person in it cannot be answered by a page about a person, and he gets there by being wrong out loud four times.** **The Void is drawn in about eleven minutes: a page that ends one document, once, on the ground that the document's own condition was not met, on a thing that claims to be the work of a person, a record and not a finding, and it does not say why.** Nine people are asked one at a time whether they will witness it; one of them, Renna Ord, is named by herself and hers to take back, and nobody took it. **Ivenn Marrow says *Single Witness* and then says *First Grammar*, four days early, in a room that was not his, without asking, and asks for all three of those things to be entered, and enters that he cannot tell afterwards whether he said them to help this city or to file it. He is not convicted of anything.** The room asks whether a named thing can be refused, and is told that a name is a description, a description is the column at the end of the page, a column is a roll, and a roll goes to Aurel four times a year — **and a woman on nothing says that this city has spent ninety-one days refusing to be described and has just described the thing that was doing the describing.** Nothing was gained.

### Chapter 0092 — What the Instrument's Own Condition Was
Day 92, the Tuesday, the second day of the month after this one. **The argument about the nineteen words is had in about two hundred and forty people and is not a victory: a man of forty-two is right that a person at the top of a thing is a man in a wet coat with a name on a list nine hundred miles away, and Adrian Vale says *I will do it* in four seconds without qualifying it.** **Mina Okoye says her own sentence about a hold, about a man who is not her, and he asks her whether she meant it and nobody else is permitted to ask on his behalf; her gloss is that if a page has a person at the top of it and nobody is asked whether that person will have it, the person is a working.** The twenty-sixth field is answered by a person, out loud, in the local language, in four seconds. **The undertaking of the Lantern Concord is void in about nineteen minutes on the ground that its own condition was not met, which is in its own first sentence, and nobody was convicted of anything and the nail is unmarked and the string untouched.** The Concord keeps the lock, the roll, the notary and the gauge and gains a term it never had: a day on it that it did not choose. **A woman of seventy asks a man of sixty-one whether he understood what he was copying, and nobody had asked him in nineteen years.** A form of four lines is finished and gets a person at the top of it for the first time, and the person is nobody.

### Chapter 0093 — What It Cost, in About Nine Minutes
Day 93, the Wednesday, the third day of the month after this one. **A man who performed a working for the first time in his life cannot get to the top of a river wall in about four minutes, and in the lower gallery he finds out in about nine minutes that what a working cost him is a person and that the person is him.** He perceives a third line that goes eleven inches from the dressed edge and stops. **He is Stage 2, Passage holder, in the doing and not in a ceremony, and what he lost is the knowing and not a road: the idea that if he ever wanted to go back there was a way to work it out, gone in about four minutes in a room.** Nine people are asked what he has given up; **eight can say and one cannot, and the one who cannot is a man of thirty-five whose shoulder cannot be set, in nine words — *Nobody in this city was told what he had* — and Adrian Vale writes them down and does not improve on them and refuses to put the loss in a second column.** A notary of fifty says that eight people have just said out loud where a man's life is thinner than it was yesterday, and that there is a column for where eight people are, and that it is on a slate in a gallery, and that this is the first time in ninety-three days anybody has noticed that.

### Chapter 0094 — The Fourth Day of the Month After This One
Day 94, the Thursday, the fourth day of the month after this one, which is the review date on a procedure of nine clauses and nine weaknesses. **Perry Lask is given the day she asked for in a hurry thirty-seven days before, and uses it: she does not re-answer and she does not withdraw, she refuses the second asking and not the work, and she says it before anybody asks her anything, and she will not let anybody make it a form, and she enters that the amendment is worse for the document and better for the person and that both are true at once. Nobody may ask her about it again.** **A notary of fifty enters that she drafted the procedure and drafted the amendment and may not grade her own paper, and that she believes the correct outcome is the worst one available.** A seat with a name on it is offered for the fifth time, and it is not a Crown instrument and not this city's, and a man of thirty-two offers it to himself in his own hand on the back of a copying list; the instrument says nobody, and he says **eleven words — *I cannot hold a seat. I have nothing to hold with*** — and says nothing else about himself for the rest of the volume.

### Chapter 0095 — Two Documents and a Road
Day 95, the Friday, the fifth day of the month after this one. **Two documents are put on a table in a converted salt store — the void undertaking, carried down two hundred steps by a keeper of about seventy, and a charcoal rubbing of the page in the stone, brought by a girl of seventeen — and may not be put in the same column, and a girl of seventeen tries to put them there anyway and is stopped after four lines, and the four lines are left in and nothing is struck.** **The finding of the day is a stall-keeper's: a document becomes a thing a person can be angry at when a person can be angry at it, and this city has now got two of them and cannot stop either.** A woman of twenty-nine closes the account of five days: everything this city has decided in ninety-five days happened in a room, and the rooms are the reason we can see them and not the reason we can stop them. The undertaking is in a case on a river and a hearing is about eight weeks off; nine Elms' water is not received; a form of four lines has a person at the top of it and nobody has filled it in; three of the twelve debts on a page in a stone are still debts. **The nineteenth day of the month after this one is on a slate and it is the only thing on it.** The chapter closes on a man of thirty-two alone in the lower gallery at about the ninth hour of the evening reading eight and seven-eighths off a wall four feet back with nobody reading it to him, and not filling the ruled line. **The roll-call form is broken here on purpose.**

### Chapter 0096 — Two Houses, and a Refusal in Public
Day 96, the Saturday, the sixth day of the month after this one. **A bed and a bowl for anybody travelling is used nine times in a fortnight in a house on the upper road, four of them mixed, at a price of a quarter of a pound of salt and an hour of washing, open to a list of nobody.** A man of thirty-two is in the room for forty minutes, says four words about the washing and not about the bed, is asked nothing, and a girl of seventeen enters all three facts on the page. **A man of about twenty-six who has walked eleven miles is refused a bed at the door in front of about nine people, for a reason on a page with a date on it, and about four people start to interfere and a woman of twenty-nine says *wait* out loud and stops it, and nobody overrides the refusal, and the reason is nailed to the inside of the door frame and does not describe him at all.** **On the seventh day of the month after this one he comes back and reads his own refusal out in his own words, and it is entered that he came back to read it and not to complain about it, and he enters that if it had been a different reason he would be angry and that he does not know yet whether he will be in a different place in a month.** Nobody crossed and the Narrow Mark was not opened and a person with no work is still a person with no work.

### Chapter 0097 — No Share, No Interest, and a Refusal That Cannot Be Bought Out
Day 97, the Sunday, the seventh day of the month after this one. **The Open Hand is written into a book in about four hours by fourteen people: no share, no interest, and a right of refusal that cannot be bought out, and every one of the three is expensive.** The three are said in about forty seconds without stopping by a woman of about thirty-four who keeps a stall by the wharf and who said first that she had no standing and nothing at stake. **A man of forty-two argues for about nine minutes that the third of the three cannot be administered and is right, and says in the same breath that it is the only one of the three worth having and that nobody is going to have an answer and that not having one is correct.** A keeper of about seventy gives the reason it holds: it is the first rule anybody has brought her that nobody is going to check. **A trader is refused in about four seconds and the refusal goes on a page in the woman's own words with no name and no reason on it, and the page is not a roll.** Adrian Vale is in the room for four hours, says four words, is asked nothing, is not needed, and is not party to the first institution in ninety-seven days that this city has built which he cannot hold.

### Chapter 0098 — The Quarterly Description
Day 98, the Monday, the eighth day of the month after this one. **The Concord's quarterly description of its own district goes down to a wharf in a wooden case with a strap and a wax and a second strap, and it is complete, and it is true, and it carries nothing anybody nine hundred miles away can act on, and that is the design and not an accident.** The seventh column still reads that they were described in the quarter last past and nothing has been asked of them since, and the receiving-party cell still carries a cross and nineteen words. **The eighth column, headed *person to be asked*, is answered with a question and not with a name, at the asking of a Hearthguard of forty-four, who has had a form four times in nine years and not one question and would rather be a question than a name; the question asks which gate, in which year, on whose authority the count of eight was continued after that gate came off the roll, and what the eight names are, and names nobody, and a clerk of that office may not ask a question and may not refuse one, and a person named in a question is not told the question exists.** A woman of twenty-nine says the finding: **a district which describes itself honestly is a district that will be described correctly**, and the emptiest thing in the case is the only thing anybody can act on. **The twenty-seventh field is refused in under a second by a girl of seventeen, and the count of refusals in Sallow's second book, kept at home and never shown to anybody, goes to twenty-three for the first time in eleven batches, and the standing that the wall has never once registered a refusal ends in a room over a market.** Nobody in this city says the word *budget*, and the fee is entered as owing with no date, and it has no date because a form that is honest about what it does not know has nothing to put in that column.

### Chapter 0099 — The Date on the Page Before the Day
Day 99, the Tuesday, the ninth day of the month after this one. **The trial licence comes up for the flood-season review named on its own page forty-nine days before the day, and survives it, and comes out weaker, and the thing that is weaker is the only part of it that was doing any work**: the four words *nobody has asked them* were a refusal for twenty-four days and a refusal is not entered anywhere, and they are now on a page with a heading and a date, and a dated refusal is an answer, and an answer is a description, and there is now a column for where the seventeen are with a space under the date for an officer's hand nine hundred miles away. **No opening is authorised, the pull is not restored, the seat of holder is still vacant, and nothing in this city was opened on this day.** A woman of seventy of Nine Elms says she has refused three times and that three is a thing a village can do and four is a thing a person does, and a question about what happens at four goes on the page unanswered. **The four who read the join-line disagree for the fourth time since the first of March, three at a line and Peth Lomas a sixteenth under, all four entries stand, the twenty-fifth reading is not taken and one ruled line is left in the column**, and a woman of forty-four comes four miles to sign the fresh inspection and does not enter the four miles because the column is full and she is not going to be the twelfth. The four sign, and the man who reads the number reads it off a wall four feet back and says it out loud, and the number is a number and the dressed edge is dressed.

### Chapter 0100 — Two Houses and a Road
Day 100, the Wednesday, the tenth day of the month after this one, the hundredth day of the flood. **A boundary shrine on a fork at Four Elms is given back to the household that keeps it, with a third page nailed inside it carrying a date, a price and a right of refusal, and it is the second Wayhouse, and a woman of about sixty-one argued for three pages in a stone on behalf of a stranger who will have to keep it in about four years, and the giving back is the image and not the putting in.** **The question owed to a woman of forty-eight in a fourth house at Nine Elms is entered on that page as unasked, on purpose, with a date on it, so that in four months nobody can say it was forgotten, and nobody in about nine people argued for asking her, and the reason given is that a person who has been doing a thing by herself every morning and has not been asked has not refused anything.** The road is kept by eleven households and is on no page. **Two Wayhouses and one Open Hand exchange stand under local law and none of them is Adrian Vale's.** Ivenn Marrow says the count of the letter he has not mentioned is sixty days and asks for it to be entered by somebody who is not him, and says his room is in about fourteen weeks. The volume closes on the hundredth day: **at the top of the upper road a woman of about sixty-one who cannot read reads nineteen shapes aloud to a docker who has a slate, because anybody may pick the work up and the copying is finished and the meaning is unwritten; and on a river wall a man from another world reads a number off a wall four feet back from it and says it out loud in an empty gallery, and the number is a number and not a sentence about anybody, the joined edge is dressed, there is no field in the air, and the day book says what it cannot do.**

## Volume 03, Batch 0001 — Chapters 101–110: A Case That Does Not Come Back

### Chapter 0101 — A Case That Does Not Come Back
Day 101, the Thursday, the eleventh day of the month after this one, one day after the hundredth day of the flood. **Nine people on a wet wharf work out that there is no column in the world for an answer, that this city has been protected by that for a hundred and forty years, and that the protection is that nothing comes back** — the voidness of the undertaking exists in one book in this city and the question in the eighth column of the quarterly description is in a wooden case on the same river going the same way, and no vehicle in this world carries one to the other. **A quarter is about thirteen weeks and the hearing is about eight, so the next time this city can put anything in front of that office is about five weeks after the hearing.** Nobody read the question out and it is entered that nobody did; a woman of about thirty-four who keeps a stall by the wharf says the reason, that a thing said in a language is a thing that has left your mouth. **Both names are on a page in a man's own hand and are not repeated on the wharf, and a man in a good coat says again in front of nine people that he is not going to explain either of them.** A boy of nine says he will take a thing up the nine miles again and is told the date, and says yes before he is asked, and it is written down.

### Chapter 0102 — Nine Minutes, and the Fifth Room
Day 102, the Friday, the twelfth day of the month after this one. **Nine people try for about nine minutes to find out how a page ended a document, and fail, and the failure is entered with the date on it, and the page is not opened.** Five things are tried: what the page says, which is a heading and seven lines; what it was laid on, which is a barrel, a floor and a Thursday; who was in the room, which is nine living named people and is the only part anybody can check; the document, which holds, because a thing that argues for itself is a thing you can check; and why, which is nothing. **The one sentence that came closest was not written down, because a sentence about a page that works is a mechanism and a diagram of the most dangerous object in this city is a worse thing to have than nine minutes of nothing.** The count of rooms is entered as **five with the four named**, because a city that has failed at a thing four times and has never written the four down will fail a fifth time in the same place. A docker of fifty-eight who cannot read says the reason nobody has asked the page anything is not fear, and says she has not asked it anything either and is not going to.

### Chapter 0103 — The Cost of an Answer Is a Person
Day 103, the Saturday, the thirteenth day of the month after this one. **Nine people go down two hundred steps of a river wall on a condemned rail to look at a seam that has taken two people in a hundred and three days and has never been asked a question.** The four read the join-line and **disagree for the fifth time since the first of March, three at a line and Peth Lomas a sixteenth under, all five entries stand, and the twenty-fifth reading is not taken.** A docker of fifty-eight says **the cost of a gate is the answer and the cost of an answer is a person**, in sixteen words, and a Hearthguard of forty-four says a gate is a thing that takes a person standing behind it. **A gate and a page are compared and the comparison is refused in about four seconds by the man who has done it, and the refusal is entered.** A single witness is said aloud in the local language on a step by a person who was in the room when it was named, and the other name is not said. Nothing happened for about nineteen minutes on purpose, and a mason of thirty-four years gives four reasons not to go and look, and the Narrow Mark is not opened.

### Chapter 0104 — A Room That Has Never Been Described
Day 104, the Sunday, the fourteenth day of the month after this one. **Four people finish ten days of trying to write down the room at the far end of the Narrow Mark, and the description is four lines, and every line of it is about the eleven people who have been in the room and not about the room.** A mason of thirty-four years says **a description of the people who were in a room is not a description of a room**, seventeen words, and then *and a description is not a place*, seven with the and in it, used against herself. **Curtis Okonkwo, twenty, who has said three words in seventy-nine days, says the four lines are the shape of the room after eleven people have been through it, and nobody argues.** Nobody goes and looks; **the boards were not lifted and the ground is still a scar.** A docker of fifty-eight asks **who is it about**, four words, and nobody answers, and it is entered that a notary of fifty noticed that nobody had.

### Chapter 0105 — Every Institution in This City Is a Person, and Every One of Them Is Tired
Day 105, the Monday, the fifteenth day of the month after this one. **About nine people spend four hours in a low room by the salt wharf and work out that every institution in this city is a person and every one of them is tired, and that the reason this city can be argued with is the same reason it can be worn out.** A bone-setter of fifty-eight says the figure herself — ninety wrong out of four hundred — and says a man who has been wrong ninety times out of four hundred is tired and not a fool. A woman of forty-four comes four miles, says she is owed a figure and a decision about ninety-one acres and is not here about either, asks to be left alone, and is left alone. A woman of thirty-eight on no roll says her daughter's name twenty-four days ago and asks for the not-finding-out to be entered. A notary of fifty gives the batch's second law: **a person may carry a cost, there is no other instrument, and if nobody is allowed to carry anything then in four months there are four hundred people in a city where a cost exists and no person holds it, and a cost that nobody holds is not a cost that has stopped, it is a cost that has got nobody.** **The twenty-eighth thing the wall has said is accepted in about four seconds by the bone-setter, and the refusals count does not move, and it is entered that every one of us in that room is somebody who came and were not asked anything.**

### Chapter 0106 — The Reason Nobody Can Answer
Day 106, the Tuesday, the sixteenth day of the month after this one. **The middle of the batch: two pages go side by side and the reason nobody in this city can answer the question in the eighth column is that this city has spent a hundred and six days not writing names down, and the not-writing was the protection and is now the reason.** A girl of seventeen puts a copy of the second page in the stone next to it and establishes that it is a description with a date on it, that every name on it is a place where a person is, and that **it is the only roll in this city that does not go to Aurel in a wooden case, and that the reason is a page in a stone, and that the copy is headed *it is a copy and the stone does not travel* because a book with twelve names in it is a door into a list.** The twelve are twelve people and not twelve debts. **A Hearthguard of forty-four says eighteen words before anybody puts it to him — I asked for a question instead of a name, and I have got a question I cannot answer — and asks not to be told it was the right thing to do, and nobody absolves him.** Two ways to refuse the question are found and **both are refused on purpose with the date on the entry**, and a docker of fifty-eight says **then we go and look at the seven**, and nobody argued, and it is entered that this is not a plan.

### Chapter 0107 — The Answer Is Not a Person
Day 107, the Wednesday, the seventeenth day of the month after this one. **Nine people sit in a room over a market with the only bound book anybody in this city can put a hand on, and a clerk of the Registry at Aurel is told that the answer to his own arithmetic is not a person and cannot be asked anything.** The difference between his two books was nine before the first registered refusal and is ten now. A girl of seventeen is asked for the sixth time whether she wants to know which book a field goes in and says no, and gives the reason, and it is the finding of the day. **The twenty-ninth thing the wall has said offers him a correction to a count that nobody has to sign, and he says yes in about four seconds, and then does not do it, and enters that he could change thirteen to anything tonight in a room upstairs alone and that the reason is twenty-three words.** A man of thirty-two says **the answer is not a person and that is the whole of what we know**, in fifteen words, and a notary of fifty says what it means: a number cannot be asked whether it will have it, a page cannot, a person can, **a number is safe and a page is not, and the difference is that a number cannot end anything and a page can, and there is one of the second kind in this city and it is spent.**

### Chapter 0108 — Four Days Out
Day 108, the Thursday, the eighteenth day of the month after this one. **Nine people go up a nine-mile road in the wrong weather because anybody may pick the work up, and a woman of about sixty-one who cannot read hands nine years of paper to a girl of seventeen on the second mile and says she would like somebody to have the date and not her.** A keeper of seventy stays behind for a reason about herself and is not asked. A boy of nine counts the households and gets nine and then finds a shutter and a wall and is right. **A docker of fifty-eight looking at nine years of a woman's handwriting finds two lines struck out of the oldest year, and a mason of thirty-four years says the number has not gone down and a hole in a page is a place where a number used to be, and that nobody has compared nine years of one woman's February in nine years, and it is entered today with the date on it.** The whole of what a man of thirty-two did on a road was ask a docker to read him a date off a slate. **The wall says nothing and the count is seventeen for seventeen.**

### Chapter 0109 — Seven Nobody Has Looked At
Day 109, the Friday, the nineteenth day of the month after this one, the day on the slate. **Nine people stand in the wrong weather nine miles up a road at eight cuts that are different from the eleven and look at seven of them for the first time in nine years, and can say what every one of them is and not one of them what it means.** The name is where it was, and **he was asked to read it aloud and said no to a person and not to a document, and it is entered that a document would have got a yes out of him.** Four of the seven are one hand, three are three different hands, one has an older cut under it in the hand of the eleven, one is not letters and is a figure about two inches high with a bar across the top of it that may be a number, and two are a third lower than the other five where the ground is higher and **the difference is not a third.** Six people try for an hour and a half to say what the seven mean and four get close and nobody gets there, **and the finding is that the reason is not that nobody is clever enough.** A woman of twenty-four says out loud that nobody has asked him, and **he says it is a man standing next to his grandfather's hand**, and is asked whether he will say it in a room and says **I do not know**, four words, and the first honest sentence about himself in nine years. A mason refuses to name the stone. **Nobody is going to write anything on the wall.**

### Chapter 0110 — The Count of the Seven
Day 110, the Saturday, the twentieth day of the month after this one. **Nine people come down a nine-mile road in the wrong weather with a count of seven cuts in four hands and no meaning in them, and the count goes onto the second page inside a stone at a fork, under the twelve names, under the price of salt, with the word *nobody* on the line above and a line under saying it is a count and not a debt — and the keeping household did not read it before it went in and was not asked to.** The last of the six lines is a finding and the five above it are not: **a finding about a failure is the only kind of finding this city has ever written down.** A boy of nine asks who is going to be told, and a man of thirty-two says **we will stand there**, and puts the hearing at forty-four days and names the day it was given and the day it was received, and there is one hearing and one clock. **A date on a page is a door and not a promise, and the door did not open: the date on the page inside the first Wayhouse was four days ago, the household did not close the house, and nobody was told why and nobody asked.** The four go down two hundred steps at the seventh hour and the twenty-fifth reading is not taken for the fourth time in this batch. **The last image is a number on a page in a stone that a household may take down on a date, and is not asked why twice, and nobody has asked.**

## Volume 03, Batch 0002 — Chapters 111–120: A Case That Came Up

### Chapter 0111 — The Hearing Is a Room
Day 111, the Sunday, the twenty-first day of the month after this one, the hundred and eleventh day of the flood. **The morning after the nine-mile road, and the finding of the day is a clock that runs the wrong way round:** a hearing is in forty-three days, the next case goes out at the quarter, and a quarter is about thirteen weeks, so the last time this city can put anything in front of that office is about five weeks after the room in which it would have mattered has gone home. **Nothing can be sent and nothing can be prepared, because anything prepared has to go in a book in this city or a stone nine miles up a road, and those are the two ends of it.** A man of thirty-two says the thing the morning was standing on, in about four seconds: a hearing is a room, and a room is the only instrument in four hundred miles an office nine hundred miles away cannot rewrite — and a woman of twenty-four writes *he would rather be in the room* with no marks round it. **About nine people get to a number in about nine minutes and cannot get past it: the number is nine, four people said it out loud, and it went into a column in a book with no heading and no date, because a heading is a column and a column is a roll and a roll goes in a case.** He then says the half nobody else had said — the two people who can carry nothing are the reason, and neither of them can carry anything. The four go down two hundred steps at the seventh hour, the twenty-fifth reading is not taken, and a boy of nine's question from yesterday is still unanswered.

### Chapter 0112 — A Date on a Door Is Not a Page
Day 112, the Monday, the twenty-second day of the month after this one. **THE DATE IN THE CHALK ON A VILLAGE DOOR AT NINE ELMS**, in the same hand on nine doors, two months old, not washed, and the document it came with is void and the date is on the door anyway. The notice was nailed to nine doors on the sixty-fourth day of the new month and the fourth channel is shut under the fourth line of a term sheet, which is in force and was never given up; the undertaking that would have paid for the water is void. **Ilda Marsh, seventy, of Nine Elms, refuses a fourth time as a person and not as a village: three is a thing a village can do and four is a thing a person does, and the four does what she says it does, and she has a cart at noon and would like it written down.** The water has not been received for forty-three days. A man of thirty-two reads the date off the door four feet back and says a page can be taken down on a date by the household that keeps it and a door cannot, so the mark is not a protection but a mark with no way of being answered — and a woman of about thirty-four who keeps a stall by the wharf has it exactly backwards in nine seconds: **a page can be taken down and a door cannot, and those are the same sentence with the words moved about, and one is a protection and the other is a thing nobody can do anything with.** Nobody washed it off and nobody is going to. Nobody went to a fourth house, nobody stood outside one, and nobody asked about a woman of forty-eight.

### Chapter 0113 — A Ledger Nobody Asked For
Day 113, the Tuesday, the twenty-third day of the month after this one. **THE OPEN HAND IS ASKED TO CARRY SOMETHING AND REFUSES IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, and the refusal goes on a board with a rate on it for nineteen things and no name on it.** The ask is made badly on purpose by a man of thirty-two: let a cooperative of traders with a right of refusal that cannot be bought out carry nine people nine hundred miles. The reason is that carrying them means putting nine names in a carrier's book, and a book of names is a roll, and a roll goes in a case. A man of about thirty-nine who has traded on the same nineteen rates for four years then asks to be added by name, and a woman of about thirty-four says no in about four seconds in **nineteen words — a name on this board is a name in a book, a name in a book is a column** — and gives the rest of the reason, that in this city a person is somebody you can find and in a book nine hundred miles away a person is a line. **A carrier of the second table then says the thing nobody in that room has said in a hundred and thirteen days, in nine seconds: a case with a name in it can be carried**, and that this is why the city has sent a question and a number and not eight names, and that the answer it will get is a correct number about a district with eight people not in it. The board is sixteen days old, is not a roll, and is not going to Aurel.

### Chapter 0114 — A Column with No Room in It
Day 114, the Wednesday, the twenty-fourth day of the month after this one. **FORTY MEN UNDER THE NEAR GATE HAVE NOT BEEN PAID SINCE THE FIRST OF MARCH, WHICH WAS THE SIXTIETH DAY OF THE NEW MONTH AND THE EIGHTY-FIFTH DAY OF THE FLOOD, AND THAT IS TWENTY-NINE DAYS, AND THERE ARE THIRTY-NINE OF THEM AND THEY TURN UP.** The cost column has eleven entries, no figure on the end of any of them, and a ruled line under the eleventh that is ruled and has been empty for twenty-nine days, and a woman of twenty-four refuses to say the crew are owed nothing and a man of thirty-two says the reason: the rule that stops a cost being entered is the rule that makes an unpaid man look like a man who will not come. **The four go down two hundred steps with a gauge and disagree for the sixth time since the first of March, and the sixth is not the number — it is the edge — and the twenty-fifth reading is not taken and one ruled line is still ruled and still empty.** A foreman of the day crew who has said four words under that gate every working day since the forty-sixth day of the new month did not come this morning, and a docker of fifty-eight who cannot read says **thirteen words about him: a man who has stopped coming is not a cost that has stopped** — and then the rest of it, that there is no instrument in four hundred miles for saying a thing to one man who is not in the room, and a man of sixty-eight is not going to be asked whether he will have it either.

### Chapter 0115 — A Number Is Not a Roll
Day 115, the Thursday, the twenty-fifth day of the month after this one. **ABOUT NINE PEOPLE TRY TO WORK OUT WHAT TO DO WITH A HEARING IN THIRTY-NINE DAYS, GET AS FAR AS A LIST IN NINE MINUTES, AND WORK OUT IN NINE MINUTES THAT A LIST OF WHO WILL STAND IN A ROOM IS A REMOVAL WITH A COLUMN FOR WHERE THE PERSON IS.** A man of about forty-four who keeps a stall writes twenty-two lines with nine names in a left-hand column; a girl of seventeen says it is a roll he has built in a quarter; he keeps it with the left-hand column taken off. **The number is entered at about the fourth hour of the afternoon, with a date on it, and it is nine, and it is the first page in a hundred and fifteen days with a number at the top of it and no person at the top of that** — the opposite of the most dangerous page in this city, and the same kind of page. A man of thirty-two then turns the batch's own law on a household: a household is a person, a household can be asked, a household can refuse, and nine people cannot be asked to stand in a room nine hundred miles away because every one of them is somebody's household. **A keeper of about seventy who has kept a floor for fifty-one years is asked to be the number, on a Thursday, while washing it, and says no in about four seconds without stopping, and the reason is about herself and not about the number: if the number is a person then it can be asked whether the number is right, and she has read a form four times in nine years and knows what happens the first time somebody is asked a second time.** Nobody asked her again that day.

### Chapter 0116 — A Case Comes Up a River
Day 116, the Friday, the twenty-sixth day of the month after this one. **A CASE COMES UP THE RIVER, AND NOTHING HAS COME UP THIS RIVER IN A HUNDRED AND FORTY YEARS, AND IT CROSSED IN THE MIDDLE OF THE WATER WITH THE CASE THAT WENT DOWN ON THE EIGHTH DAY OF THE MONTH AFTER THIS ONE AND NEITHER OVERTOOK THE OTHER.** Four people on the quay gave the account separately and agree, and a notary of fifty entered that four agreeing accounts are not a record. The box is a foot and a half long with a strap, a flat wax with writing round the edge of it, and a second strap that is not tied. **A man of thirty-two refuses to read the writing on the wax and reads the four lines under the seal, because a form is not a face.** The direction of the nineteenth day of the new month is a **new shape, dated, the eighth shape**, kept apart from all seven and from the Quiet Choir's design, and it says the district shall name one person to be heard and that where no person is named the district shall be described by a number. **It is not opened: a case that is opened early is a case that has been read.** The thirtieth thing the wall has said offers to let any person describe the thing and puts the cost in the right place — the description is the only account that will exist — and a notary of fifty accepts it in four seconds and enters that she did not think about it. The finding: a city that has spent a hundred and sixteen days building things nobody can check has just been handed a thing it cannot check and cannot open, and the protection it has relied on for a hundred and forty years has arrived on its own account. The notary enters that she would like to be asked before it is opened.

### Chapter 0117 — A Description of a Box
Day 117, the Saturday, the twenty-seventh day of the month after this one. **NINE PEOPLE DESCRIBE A WOODEN BOX AND EVERY LINE OF THE DESCRIPTION IS ABOUT SOMEBODY.** Four people, about four hours, a girl of seventeen's book, **six attempts, the count entered with the date on it.** Attempt one is materials, attempt two is the way the box got here, attempt three is four numbers and a column, attempt four is the first one anybody notices, attempt five is nineteen lines long and is a page with a heading and a date and the heading is *the box as received*, and attempt six is abandoned. **Sixteen of the nineteen lines in the fifth attempt are about where a person was standing, who touched it, and who read the seal, and three are about the box.** The batch says the finding out loud: this city has already done this, in four lines, about a room at the far end of a seam. **A notary of fifty asks the question nobody can answer, four words, and nobody answers it.** A man of sixty-one who has copied other men's sentences for nineteen years asks to see the wax, is refused in four seconds, and gives the reason in his own words — that he has never been able to put down a sentence once he had copied it — and a woman of about thirty-four tells him he has found a better version of a sentence she said on a wharf and is not going to claim it.

### Chapter 0118 — Only Arriving
Day 118, the Sunday, the twenty-eighth day of the month after this one, the hundred and eighteenth day of the flood. **THE EMOTIONAL PAYOFF.** A boy of nine's answer to a question asked on the twenty-fifth day of the new month, which was the fiftieth day of this flood — whether the next ones are going to be players — is written on a slate in his own hand and read out by a girl of seventeen because he cannot be sure of the local hand, and it is **sixty-eight days late**, and nobody improved on it, and it settles nothing. It is two blocks: twenty-six words that answer the question and do not know, and **twenty-eight words that answer a different one — every one of you was somewhere before you were here, that is not coming, that is only arriving.** Nobody answered it, and a man of thirty-two said nothing about it for four minutes. **The chapter also separates three questions that had been run together: the three words on a stair on the thirty-fifth day of the new month were asked by Curtis Okonkwo, twenty, of the second list, and not by the boy; the boy's question of the twenty-fifth of the new month is this one; and the six words he asked on the sixth mile of the nine-mile road on the twentieth day of the month after this one were answered by a man of thirty-two in about four seconds in Chapter 0110 and the answer is on a slate and in no book and has never been entered anywhere.** He has four slates; the answer is on the flat of one of his three and the six words are on the back of another of them, and a girl of seventeen says so out loud when she is asked how she knows it is the same slate: *it is not.* **A woman of about thirty-four says in nineteen seconds that in about eight weeks somebody will get up and give a speech about what this city built, and that every word of it is going to be about us, and asks for it entered that she is not going to use the boy's sentence and is not going to let anybody else use it.** A woman of twenty-four enters in advance the shame of being the person who would use it. A man of sixty-one, who had a sentence ready nineteen yards in front of the boy, is asked in a room for the first time, and a docker of fifty-eight says **a person who has never been asked is not a person who has refused**, and about four people understand in four seconds that the sentence was never about him. He is not thanked and does not expect to be. **The last beat is that nobody said anything to him about having been asked and he said nothing about having been asked.**

### Chapter 0119 — The Reading
